# Shri Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library, Jammu

**No.** <term>859 — Gha</term>

**Title** <term>The Jewel-Garland of Astrology</term> <note>original: "Jyotisharatnamala"</note>

**Author** <term>Shripati Bhatta</term> <note>A renowned 11th-century Indian mathematician and astronomer.</note>

**Extent** <term>66</term> <note>Likely referring to the number of leaves or folios in the manuscript.</note> ......................... **Age** <term>1780</term> <note>This likely refers to the year 1780 in the Vikram Samvat calendar, which corresponds to approximately 1723 CE.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology and Astronomy</term> <note>original: "Jyotish." In the Indian tradition, this term covers both the mathematical science of astronomy and the predictive science of astrology.</note>

<term>The Jewel-Garland of Astrology</term> <note>original: "Jyotisharatnamala"; a famous 11th-century treatise by the polymath Shripati, covering both astronomy and astrology.</note>, <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: "Jyotish"; the traditional Indian science of the stars, used for timekeeping and divination.</note>, <term>Folios</term> <note>original: "Patrani"; refers to the individual leaves of the manuscript, which in this case are 66 in total.</note>

--- Page 4 ---
<meta>This page follows the cataloging leaf and marks the formal commencement of the text itself. It begins with the traditional invocation (Mangalacharana) and Shripati’s introduction to his work, the "Jewel-Garland of Astrology."</meta>

The master Shripati Bhatta <note>Shripati (c. 1019–1066) was a renowned Indian mathematician and astronomer who wrote extensively on astrology and arithmetic.</note> bows to the three divisions of Time. I bow to that Lord of Time, who is the cause of the birth and dissolution of the universe, and who is the refuge of the planets. Even the yogis meditate upon that supreme reality, who is the beginning, middle, and end of all knowledge... from which <unclear>thence</unclear>...

---

### ॥ 60 ॥ Hail! Salutations to the auspicious Lord Ganesha <note>Ganesha is the elephant-headed deity traditionally invoked at the start of any Hindu manuscript to remove obstacles.</note>

I bow to the Lord of Time, the sovereign ruler, who is the primary cause of the creation and dissolution of the universe. He is the supreme reality whom even the most advanced yogis meditate upon, seeking to understand the mystery of birth, the middle state of existence, and its eventual cessation. <margin>1</margin> Having offered my salutations, I focus my mind upon the Lord of the Planets and Time. ॥1॥

Having studied the various scriptures <note>original: "shastra"; refers here to the technical scientific and divinatory texts of the era.</note> composed by the great sages such as Garga and others <note>Garga is one of the semi-legendary founding fathers of Indian astrology.</note>, I now compose this *Jewel-Garland of Astrology* <note>original: "Jyotir-ratnamala"</note>. It is written for the purpose of being worn as an "ornament around the neck" <note>The author uses a poetic metaphor: the knowledge is so beautiful and essential it should be kept as close as a necklace.</note> by those who know the stars. ॥2॥

In the <term>Shaka era</term> <note>The Shaka era is a historical calendar system starting in 78 CE, commonly used in Indian astronomical manuscripts.</note> <unclear>3 ka naya yuga ruta ghu sha</unclear>... <unclear>the number nine along with the age (yuga) and arrows (shara)</unclear>... <unclear>taking the zero point</unclear>... <unclear>the years beginning with Prabhava 8 53</unclear> <note>original: "Prabhava"; the first year in the traditional 60-year Jupiter cycle. The numbers following are likely astronomical calculations for the specific date of the manuscript's composition or example.</note>.

<note>This manuscript contains significant water damage at the edges, yet the primary list of the 60-year cycle remains legible. In Indian astrology, each of these sixty years (a <term>Samvatsara</term>) is believed to have a specific character—such as 'Bahudhanya' signifying a year of agricultural plenty or 'Virodhi' signifying a year of conflict.</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Jovian calendar (the 60-year cycle) introduced on the previous page. While the previous page listed specific year names like Prabhava and Vibhava, this folio shifts focus to the internal structure of the cycle—grouping years into "Yugas" (eras) of five years each—and identifying the divine beings who govern these periods and the celestial movements of the sun.</meta>

<margin>Prosperity 2 2 <note>original: "Shri"; an auspicious invocation frequently repeated in the margins of manuscripts to ensure the successful completion of the work.</note></margin>
Steadfastness, Year, the Divine Sage, the Arising <note>original: "Prabhava"</note>; thus seven are born by name <gloss>2</gloss>. At that time, there are 12 <term>Yugas<note>In this context, a Yuga is a five-year sub-cycle used in ancient Vedic astronomy to synchronize lunar and solar years.</note></term> occurring in five-year intervals; indeed, there are twelve such eras in the sixty years. ॥ I shall now speak of their presiding deities in order, as ordained by the Sages <gloss>13</gloss>: Vishnu <note>The Preserver</note>, Jiva <note>Jupiter/Brihaspati</note>, Shakra <note>Indra, King of Gods</note>, and Dahana <note>Fire/Agni</note>, as well as Shashi <note>The Moon</note>, Ahirbudhnya <note>The Serpent of the Deep</note>, the Pitris <note>The Ancestral Spirits</note>, the Vishvedevas <note>The Universal Gods</note>, Soma <note>The Nectar of the Moon</note>, Tryambaka <note>The Three-Eyed Shiva</note>, and Jvalana <note>The Burning Fire</note>.
<margin>Om <note>The sacred syllable representing the sound of the universe.</note></margin>
The Nasatyas <note>The twin Ashvin physicians of the gods</note>, and Bhaga <note>The god of fortune</note> <gloss>14</gloss>. The <term>Samvatsara<note>The first year of the five-year sub-cycle.</note></term> is the first; [then] the *Parivatsara*, the *Idavatsara*, the *Anuvatsara*, and the *Vatsara*—thus others declare them to be. In all these eras, their respective lords are to be worshipped: the Moon <note>original: "Shitagu"; literally "the cool-rayed one"</note> and Brahma <note>original: "Virinchi"; the Creator</note> in their proper order. ॥ 15 ॥ The three seasons beginning with the Cool Season <note>original: "Shishira"; roughly mid-January to mid-March</note> are called the **Northern Path** <note>original: "Uttarayana"</note>; that time constitutes the **Day of the Gods**. <margin>Shiva 2</margin>
The **Southern Path** <note>original: "Dakshinayana"</note> is declared to be the [remaining] three seasons; that is the **Night of the Gods** <gloss>16</gloss>. Entering a new home, the consecration of deities, marriage, the tonsure ceremony, the taking of vows, and all manner of piercings <note>Refers to *Karnavedha*, or ear-piercing ceremonies.</note>...

<summary>This page outlines the division of the 60-year Jovian cycle into twelve 5-year Yugas, listing the deities presiding over each and defining the Northern and Southern celestial paths of the sun as the day and night of the gods.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the naming of specific years in the Jovian cycle to the broader divisions of astronomical time. It details the two solstices (ayanas), the six seasons (ritus) and their divine protectors, and finally defines the four traditional methods of measuring a month in the Indian calendar system.</meta>

In the Northern Solstice <note>original: "Saumyayane"; also known as Uttarayana, the half of the year when the sun moves northward, traditionally considered a highly auspicious time for rituals.</note> auspicious actions are prescribed; 
indeed, specific actions are beneficial in the Southern [Solstice] <note>original: "Dakshinayana"</note> as well. ॥ 17 ॥ म <note>catchword for the next section</note>
From the Sun’s transit through a pair of zodiac signs <note>original: "Rashi"</note> ॥ 
six <unclear>seasons</unclear> arise: the Dewy season <note>Shishira; late winter</note>, Spring <note>Vasanta</note> ॥ 
Summer <note>Grishma</note>, the Rainy season <note>Varsha</note>, and Autumn <note>Sharad</note> likewise ॥ 
and that called Winter <note>Hemanta; early winter</note>—thus the six are spoken of. 
॥ 18 ॥ The Wind-gods <note>Maruts</note>, Fire <note>Agni</note>, the Lord of the Thirty [Indra] <note>original: "Tridashapati"; referring to the King of the Gods who rules over the thirty-three deities.</note>, 
the Universal Gods <note>Vishvedevas</note>, the Lord of Procreation <note>Prajapati</note>, and Vishnu ॥ 
These, in order, starting from the Dewy season, 
are remembered as the Lords of the Seasons. ॥ 19 ॥ दर्शा <note>catchword: "Darsha," the New Moon</note>
The <term>Lunar month<note>Chandra-masa; a month calculated based on the phases of the moon, specifically the time between two new moons or two full moons.</note></term> is desired [to be] the period ending with the New Moon; the <term>Solar month<note>Saura-masa; a month based on the Sun’s entry into a new zodiac sign, used to determine the solar year.</note></term> [is calculated] 
likewise from the Sun’s transit through a zodiac sign. ॥ The [period of] thirty 
days is named <term>Savana<note>A "civil" month consisting of exactly thirty daily sunrises, used for practical administrative purposes and certain sacrificial durations.</note></term> by the noble ones; the <term>Sidereal month<note>Nakshatra-masa; the time it takes for the moon to complete one full circuit through the 27 lunar mansions or stars.</note></term> 
is the Moon’s [passage] through the circle of the stars <note>original: "mrigachakra"; literally "the path of the deer," a poetic term for the zodiac.</note>. ॥ 20 ॥ 
At the rising of the Sun... the lunar... <note>This line marks the beginning of a definition of the lunar day or Tithi, but the folio ends mid-sentence.</note>

...in [secular] actions. The <term>Civil month<note>original: "Savana"; a month of exactly 30 days, used for administrative purposes and calculating the duration of specific religious vows.</note></term> is considered [prescribed] in all vows and similar rituals. ॥ 21 ॥

Madhu, Madhava, Shukra, and Shuchi; Nabhas and Nabhasya; 
likewise Isha, Urja, Sahas, and Sahasya; Tapas 
and Tapasya—these are the [Vedic] names [of the twelve months] in their proper sequence. <note>These archaic Vedic names correspond to the pairs of months within the six seasons: Spring (Madhu, Madhava), Summer (Shukra, Shuchi), Monsoon (Nabhas, Nabhasya), Autumn (Isha, Urja), Winter (Sahas, Sahasya), and the Dewy Season (Tapas, Tapasya).</note> ॥ 22 ॥

The months share the names of their presiding constellations <note>original: "Nakshatras"; the months are named based on which star the full moon occurs near, such as Chaitra being named after the star Chitra.</note>. 
The two fortnights are assigned to the Gods and the Ancestors respectively. 
Indeed, the Bright and Dark fortnights are established; 
they are praised for [the performance of] auspicious and inauspicious actions.

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the Hindu calendar. Having previously defined months and fortnights, the text now focuses on the individual lunar days (Tithis), their divine patrons, and their categorical classifications.</meta>

### Lord Harkart <note>Likely referring to a patron or a specific regional title associated with this copy of the manuscript.</note>

The horses <note>original: vājin; often referring to the seven horses that pull the Sun's chariot</note> who bestow radiance; Varuna <note>the deity of the celestial ocean and cosmic law</note>;
<gloss>the 12 Sun-gods</gloss> the embodiments of Dharma and Shiva; the sharp-rayed Sun; the God of Love; Kali <margin>the Kali Age</margin>
and thereafter even within the sunbeams. ॥ 1 ॥ In the lunar day <note>original: Tithi</note> known as the [specific] direction, [one honors] the Ancestors, the compassionate ones, and the Lords... the thirteenth
<gloss>8</gloss> for the two... <gloss>9</gloss> <gloss>5</gloss> known as well-remembered
<gloss>3</gloss> <gloss>2</gloss> <gloss>1</gloss> <gloss>9</gloss> <gloss>7</gloss> <gloss>5</gloss> <gloss>6</gloss> <gloss>5</gloss> ... Fire <note>original: Vahni; the god Agni</note>, the Creator <note>original: Virinchi; Brahma</note>, the Lord of the Mountain <note>original: Giri; Shiva</note>, and the Lord of the World <note>original: Jagalesha; Ganesha</note>.
The Serpent <note>Phani; the Naga spirits</note>, Kartikeya <note>original: Vishakha</note>, the Maker of Day <note>original: Dinakrit; the Sun</note>, and Great Indra.
<gloss>11</gloss> <gloss>11</gloss> <gloss>12</gloss> <gloss>13 God of Love</gloss> <gloss>14</gloss> <gloss>15</gloss> 
Durga, the Finisher <note>original: Antaka; Yama, the god of death</note>, Vishnu, Hari, the God of Love <note>original: Smara</note>,

On the "Joyful" <note>Nanda</note> days, one should conduct colorful festivals, manage looms or fields <note>original: tantra-kshetra; referring to both weaving and agriculture</note>, and likewise perform dances.
Weddings, matters concerning kings, and the preparation of ceremonial carts or banners—these tasks are successfully completed on "Joyful" days. ॥ 7 ॥

On the "Victorious" <note>Jaya</note> days, activities involving battle or the use of physical strength
<margin>Shiva 4</margin> reach their successful conclusion once they are begun.
On the "Empty" <note>Rikta</note> days, works involving cunning, killing, striking, or the use of weapons reach their fulfillment.

### || 8 || All Auspicious Marriage Processions

The seventeenth [unit of time] is when one should perform pacification rituals <term>Shantik Karma</term> <note>ceremonies performed to avert calamity or balance spiritual energies</note>. One must always remain free from the ten ancestral rites <term>Pitru Karma</term> <note>rituals dedicated to deceased ancestors, generally considered inauspicious for celebratory events like weddings</note>; do not perform other rituals unless the timing is known to be auspicious and joyful. Those lunar days <term>Tithi</term> that are even-numbered <note>original: sugma</note> are considered favorable, whereas the "holes" <note>original: randhra; referring to the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days which are often avoided</note> should be omitted. Except for the ninth <term>Navami</term> day, the sages have declared these times suitable, with the tenth <term>Dashami</term> being particularly carried forth as auspicious. ॥ 10 ॥

On the second lunar day <term>Dvitiya</term>, when the sun alternates positions, or when the moon resides in the sign of Sagittarius <term>Dhanu</term> or Pisces <note>original: saphara; literally 'the large fish'</note>... [the text describes specific planetary alignments]. Success is certainly attained when the moon is in Cancer <term>Karki</term> or Aries <note>original: me; abbreviation for Mesha</note>, following the movement of the planets through the junctions of the signs. ॥ 11 ॥

Regarding the first four [days] and including the fifth <term>Panchami</term>, one circle [of time] is completed <unclear>even if</unclear>. Whether it be the day after or the one following that, the lunar day falling under the "arm" of a cruel zodiac sign <note>original: krura rashi; referring to signs governed by planets like Mars or Saturn that are considered harsh in Vedic astrology</note> must be accounted for.

### || 12 || The Waning Moon and the First Lunar Day

<meta>This page transitions from the previous discussion on planetary rituals and prohibitions into technical mathematical astronomy (Siddhanta). It specifically addresses the calculation of the "Start of the Day" (Varapravritti). It establishes Lanka as the Prime Meridian (zero-longitude) and explains how to adjust the time of sunrise based on a local observer's position using two key factors: Deshantara (longitude) and Chararddha (half ascensional difference).</meta>

...and it was not clearly explained for the distinct movement [of the planets] as stated above. || 10 ||

The sages say that the start of the weekday [begins] from the moment of sunrise in the capital of Ravana <note>original: Ravana-rajadhanyam; refers to the city of Lanka, which in ancient Indian astronomy serves as the Prime Meridian where the equator meets the central meridian</note>. This [time] is adjusted higher or lower from that point by the *nadikas* <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> of the half ascensional difference and the difference in longitude. || 11 ||

Having properly calculated the subtraction or addition of the half ascensional difference <term>Chararddha</term> <note>the time difference between the actual sunrise at a location and the sunrise on the equator</note> and the longitude <term>Deshantara</term> <note>the distance east or west of the prime meridian</note>, the lords of the sages <unclear>chiefs</unclear> have declared the start of the weekday [to be determined] after the time of sunrise. || 12 ||

In the Northern Hemisphere <note>original: saumya-gole</note>, the lords of the sages say the start of the weekday occurs after the rise of the Sun, <gloss>likewise</gloss> [it is adjusted] by the half-difference of the solar measures in the Southern Hemisphere. That [value], multiplied by the longitude and divided by four <note>original: veda; the number four is often represented by the word 'Veda' in Sanskrit astronomical notation</note>, [is used] to certainly determine the middle of the earth... [for those in] the West...

# The Solar Sequence of the Threefold Paths
<note>original: Trivata Saura Dashamanuchah; this title likely refers to a specific system of solar or astrological classification used to determine the outcomes of actions</note>

<margin>
Prosperity
Ra
7

...having bathed at the center of the village. || 13 || <note>original: gramadham snate; the context is partially obscured but likely refers to a purification ritual required before commencing the works mentioned hereafter</note>

On the weekday <term>Vara</term> belonging to a specific planet, any work undertaken when that planet is in its exalted or increasing state will surely attain success <term>Siddhi</term>. Conversely, that which is undertaken during its declining phase <note>original: apasavyavahasya; referring to an unfavorable or retrograde-like influence</note> will remain unaccomplished, even if one exerts great effort in its construction. || 14 ||

Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday are the weekdays that grant success in all auspicious <term>Shubha</term> activities. On Sunday, Tuesday, and Saturday, however, success in one's actions is achieved only through harsh or vigorous methods. || 15 || <note>original: Soma (Monday/Moon), Saumya (Wednesday/Mercury), Guru (Thursday/Jupiter), Shukra (Friday/Venus), Bhanu (Sunday/Sun), Bhauma (Tuesday/Mars), Shani (Saturday/Saturn)</note>

For the dyeing of materials with lac-resin, madder, or safflower; and for the Son of Earth <note>original: Mahisuta; an epithet for the planet Mars</note> who rules over gold, kings, <margin> Shiva <br> Ra </margin> and minerals <note>original: gaga-sunuh; literally "sons of the mountains"</note>, as well as the working of iron. || 16 || <note>This verse associates specific materials and trades—specifically red dyes and metals like gold and iron—with the planetary influence of Mars, suggesting these activities are best performed on his day</note>

For the <term>commencement of education</term> <note>original: Vidyarambha; the formal beginning of a child's studies</note>, the <term>Teacher of the Gods</term> <note>original: Suraguru; Jupiter</note>, the <term>Bright One</term> <note>original: Sita; Venus</note>, and the <term>Knowing One</term> <note>original: Jna; Mercury</note> are the givers of desired success. --- Page 24 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Vara-prakaranam" (Chapter on the Days of the Week) and begins the sequence of the twenty-seven "Yogas," which are astrological time-divisions based on the longitudinal sum of the Sun and the Moon. It bridges the concept of planetary hours (Horas) with the broader auspicious combinations used in the Hindu calendar (Panchangam).</meta>

One should know the divisions of the day; the lords of the hours (<term>Horas</term>) <note>original: horāpatayaḥ; planetary hours roughly equivalent to one twenty-fourth of a day</note> are known in groups of five. Beginning with the ruler of the current day, the lords are the Sun, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars in their respective order. <note>original: ravi-śukra-saumya-śaśāṅka-sauri-ijya-kujāḥ; this follows the "Chaldean" order used in both Indian and Western astrology</note> Whatever ritual or action is prescribed for the day of a particular planet, all of that should be performed during the specific hour (Hora) ruled by that same planet. || 24 ||

->**Thus ends the third chapter, "On the Days," in the *Jewel-Garland of Astronomers* composed by the venerable Śrīpati Bhaṭṭa.**<-

In sequence <meta>ink smudge</meta> the <term>Yogas</term> <note>original: Nitya Yogas; twenty-seven mathematical combinations of the Sun and Moon used to determine the character of a day</note> occur: <term>Viṣkumbha</term> ("The Threshold"), <term>Prīti</term> ("Fondness"), and after that <term>Āyuṣmān</term> ("Long-lived"). Then <term>Saubhāgya</term> ("Good Fortune"), <term>Śobhana</term> ("Splendor"), <term>Atigaṇḍa</term> ("Great Obstacle"), and also <term>Sukarma</term> ("Virtuous Action"). || 1 || 

Thus the results of <term>Gaṇḍa</term> <note>original: Gaṇḍa-yoga; one of the inauspicious yogas suggesting difficulties</note> <meta>ink smudge</meta>

One should arrange thirteen [lines or points] placed transversely; this is the diagram <term>Chakra</term> <note>original: cakra; a wheel or mystical diagram used for calculations</note> declared by the wise to be like the rays of the Sun. Starting from the <term>Vyāghāta</term> yoga <note>The "Striking" yoga mentioned on the previous page</note> and others located within the diagram, when the Sun and Moon are in a specific alignment, it is called <term>Ekārgala</term> <note>original: ekārgala; literally "a single bolt" or "wooden bar used to lock a door." All auspiciousness <unclear>is destroyed</unclear>; with a fiery, tawny gaze, it utterly burns the world. || 8 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of Nakshatras (lunar mansions) according to their qualities (gunas). While the previous page focused on the physical orientation (where the stars "look"), this section categorizes them by their inherent nature—such as "Fixed," "Soft," or "Sharp"—to determine which human activities will succeed under their influence. This system remains a cornerstone of elective astrology (Muhurta) used to time weddings, construction, and rituals.</meta>

### The Classification of Lunar Mansions

The three <note>referring to the three stars named "Uttara"</note> along with <term>Rohini</term> are known by the sages as <unclear>the fixed ones</unclear>. In these <term>Sthira</term> <note>Fixed or Permanent</note> stars, one should perform deeds intended for long-term stability: the sowing of seeds, the founding of houses and cities, royal consecrations, the planting of groves, and rites for peace and the cultivation of the intellect.
|| 7 ||

The stars presided over by the deities <term>Tvashtar</term> <note>Chitra nakshatra</note>, <term>Mitra</term> <note>Anuradha nakshatra</note>, and the <term>Moon</term> <note>Mrigashira nakshatra</note>, along with <term>Revati</term>, are declared by the sages to be the <term>Mridu</term> <note>Soft or Gentle</note> group. These are auspicious for the works of friendship, the enjoyment of pleasures, the wearing of ornaments, choosing a spouse, music, and the performance of holy ceremonies.
|| 8 ||

<term>Ashwini</term>, <term>Pushya</term>, and <term>Hasta</term> <note>governed by the Sun deity Arka</note> are considered the four <note>This often includes Abhijit in some systems</note> <term>Laghu</term> <note>Light or Swift</note> stars. These grant success in the administration of medicine, the acquisition of knowledge, the practice of fine arts and crafts, and in setting out upon journeys.

The stars associated with the deities <term>Shiva</term> <note>Ardra nakshatra</note> and the <term>Serpents</term> <note>Ashlesha nakshatra</note>, along with others <note>typically Jyeshtha and Mula</note>, are known by the name <term>Tikshna</term> <note>Sharp or Dreadful</note>. These are used for the taking of vows, the practice of secret mantras, and for acts involving imprisonment, weapons, or destruction.
|| 1 ||

The three <term>Vaishva</term> <note>meaning the three "Uttara" stars or specifically Uttarashadha</note> combined with <term>Punarvasu</term> and <term>Svati</term> <note>the Wind-star</note> form the five <term>Chara</term> <note>Movable or Mobile</note> stars. In these, matters concerning horses, elephants, vehicles, the planting of gardens, and travel are highly praised by the wise.
|| 1 ||

The three <term>Purva</term> stars <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note> along with...

<summary>This page classifies the Nakshatras into functional groups—Fixed, Soft, Swift, Sharp, and Movable—prescribing specific activities like farming, marriage, or travel for each category.</summary>

# Page 19
## Section: Auspicious Stars for Fire, Medicine, and Wells

In <term>Puṣya</term>, <term>Punarvasu</term>, <term>Dvidaiva</term> <note>original: "Dvidaiva" (Two-Deitied), referring to the star Viśākhā, governed by both Indra and Agni</note>, <term>Jyeṣṭhā</term>, <term>Aśvidaiva</term> <note>The star Aśvinī</note>, and the <term>Kṛttikās</term>: the **establishment of the sacred fire** <note>original: "Agnyādhāna," a foundational Vedic ritual for householders</note> is declared to be auspicious. <margin><unclear>tri</unclear></margin> In the matter of discipline and in the [aforementioned] three, it is also declared by the leading <term>Brahmins</term>. **31** 

Even in the five stars starting with <term>Mṛga</term> <note>Mṛgaśīrṣa</note>, and in <term>Mūla</term>, the three stars starting with <term>Hasta</term>, and in the <term>Aśvinīs</term>; <margin>na</margin> likewise in the three <term>Pūrvās</term> <note>Pūrvaphālgunī, Pūrvāṣāḍhā, and Pūrvabhādrapadā</note>, in <term>Śravaṇa</term>, and in <term>Śatatadvidhā</term> <note>The star Śatabhiṣaj</note>, the wise desire the commencement of any undertaking for the sake of attaining success. **32**

In the two stars of <term>Aditi</term> <note>Punarvasu</note>, in the three stars of <term>Hasta</term>, in the three stars of <term>Aśvinī</term>, in <term>Maitra</term> <note>Anurādhā</note>, in <term>Mūla</term>, and in <term>Mṛga</term>: the virtuous sages proclaim that **medical treatment** <note>original: "bhaiṣajyakarma," including the preparation or consumption of medicine</note> is highly commended. **33**

**42** Under the stars of <term>Aditi</term> <note>original: "Aditi," referring to the star Punarvasu</note>, on even lunar days, and on <term>Wednesdays</term> <note>original: "bu," abbreviation for Budhavāra</note> in <term>Viśākhā</term> and **Rohiṇī** stars: <margin>20</margin> a man performing **vows and charity** <note>original: "vratadāna," the ritual act of taking a religious oath and giving gifts</note> [will be successful]. [The following stars are auspicious for weddings or general welfare]: **Hasta**, **Aśvinī**, **Puṣya**, the <term>Uttarās</term> <note>the three 'latter' stars: Uttaraphālgunī, Uttarāṣāḍhā, and Uttarabhādrapadā</note>, **Rohiṇī**, **Citrā**, **Anurādhā**, **Maghā**, **Revatī**, **Svāti**, and **Dhaniṣṭhā**. The **Blind** <term>andhaka</term> <note>a classification of lunar mansions where the "sight" is considered obstructed, paradoxically making it easier to find lost items</note>, followed by the **Dim-sighted** <note>original: "manda-lochana"</note>, then the **Middle-sighted** <note>original: "madhyama-lochana"</note>, and after that the **Good-sighted** <note>original: "sulochana"</note>. <gloss>Further</gloss> 

Recovery of wealth occurs in the **Blind** <note>original: "andhake"</note> star; the property stolen by thieves is found very nearby. In the **Dim-sighted** star <margin>divided</margin> it is recovered with great effort. In the **Middle-sighted** star <margin>lost</margin>, the recovery is distant. Wealth is obtained most excellently through the **Good-sighted** <note>original: "sulochana"</note> star at once. **62**

Wealth returns to the owner; if stolen during a **Blind** <note>original: "andha"</note> star, it is indeed recovered. However, in the **Dim-sighted** <note>original: "manda-dṛṣṭe"</note> star, it is not found even with great effort. In the **Middle-sighted** <note>original: "madhyama-lochana"</note> stars, the object is far away. Indeed, it is never heard of again if lost during the **Good-sighted** <note>original: "sulochana"</note> stars. **63** 

If an object is stolen during the **Blind** star, it is obtained; if in the **Dim-eyed** <note>original: "manda-netra"</note> star, effort is required...

<summary>This page details a divinatory system for locating stolen property based on four categories of stars: Blind, Dim-sighted, Middle-sighted, and Good-sighted. ...obtained; if in the **Dim-eyed** <note>original: "manda-netra"</note> star, it is far away; in the **Middle-eyed** <note>original: "madhyama-netra"</note> star, there is some hope; but in the **Good-eyed** <note>original: "sunetra"</note> star, there is a certain gain within the family category. **64**

Similarly, **Anuradha** <note>original: "anurādhā"; a lunar mansion associated with friendship and success</note> and **Tishya** <note>original: "tiṣyau"; another name for the auspicious star Pushya</note>, as well as the **Ancestral Star** <note>original: "pitṛ dhiṣmye"; referring to the Nakshatra Magha</note> and **Rohini** <note>the "Red One," a star associated with growth</note>, when occurring on the day of the **Son of the Sun** <note>original: "arka-tanaye"; refers to the planet Saturn (Shani) and Saturday</note>, these result in **Success Unions** <term>Siddhi Yogas; specific alignments believed to guarantee the successful completion of a task</term>. **8**

When the numbers of the **Lunar Days** <term>Tithis; the 30 phases of the moon</term> and the **Weekdays** are combined, and the resulting sum is thirteen, it creates a union known as **The Saw** <note>original: "kakacha"; literally 'a saw,' symbolizing a cutting or destructive influence</note>. This combination must certainly be avoided in all **Auspicious Works**. **9**

The **Joyful** <note>original: "nandā"; the 1st, 6th, and 11th lunar days</note>, the **Fortunate** <note>original: "bhadrā"; the 2nd, 7th, and 12th lunar days</note>, and the **Victorious** <note>original: "jayā"; the 3rd, 8th, and 13th lunar days</note>—if these occur on the day of the **Son of the Earth** <note>original: "lohitāñje"; referring to the planet Mars</note>, or if the **Empty Days** <note>original: "riktā"; the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, usually considered inauspicious</note> occur on the day of **Saturn** <note>original: "mande"</note>, or if the **Victorious** and **Empty** days align with the day of the **Son of the Moon** <note>original: "saumyā"; referring to the planet Mercury</note>... these become **Success Days** <note>original: "siddhi tithi"</note> for all manner of actions. **10**

When the **Seventh Lunar Day** falls on the **Day of the Sun** <note>Sunday</note>, or if the **First Lunar Day** <note>original: "pratipati"</note> falls on the day of the **Son of the Moon** <note>Wednesday</note>, such a combination has been declared and pointed out by the **Sages** as truly producing **Auspicious Results**...

**Conflicting Unions** <term>Viruddha Yogas; alignments of stars and weekdays that create opposition</term> are produced by the specific combinations of the lunar mansions and the weekdays. These should not be declared to possess the six-fold strength. In the regions of the **Hala**, the **Vajra**, and the **Khasa** <note>original: "haleṣu vajreṣu khaseṣu"; these refer to ancient geographical territories—the Khasa are historically linked to the Himalayan foothills, while the others likely refer to the northwest and eastern regions of the Indian subcontinent</note>, these combinations must be avoided; however, in all other lands, they are not forbidden. **12**

In this way, when the radiance of the Sun, the **Lord of the Day** <note>original: "dinādhipo"; the planet ruling the specific weekday</note>, the sacred syllable <term>Mantra</term>, and the **Lunar Day** <term>Tithi</term> are all joined together, any person who is born under this **Union** <note>original: "gale 'smin"; likely a transcription variant for "yoge 'smin," meaning "in this astrological alignment"</note> is declared by the **Great Sages** <note>original: "munīndraiḥ"</note> to be the most famous of all. **13** <insert>Hi.</insert> Thus—

Regarding the **Sun’s Transit** <term>Sankranti; the moment the Sun enters a new sign of the Zodiac</term>: if it occurs on the day of the **Sun** (Sunday), the energy is called **Terrible** <note>original: "ghora"</note>. On the day of the **Moon** (Monday), it is **The Crow-like** <note>original: "dhvānkṣī"</note>. On the day of the **Son of Earth** (Tuesday), it is **The Big-Bellied** <note>original: "mahodarī"</note>. On the day of the **Learned One** (Wednesday), it is known as **The Slow One** <note>original: "mandā"</note>. On the day of the **Teacher** (Thursday), it is **The River of Heaven** <note>original: "mandākinī"</note>. On the day of **Venus** (Friday), it is **The Mixed One** <note>original: "miśrā"</note>. Finally, on the day of the **Son of Saturn**, it is **The Demonic** <note>original: "rākṣasī"</note>. **1**

Through the star groups classified as fierce, swift, mobile, friendly, fixed, mixed, and harsh, the names of the **Sun’s Transit** <term>Sankranti; the entry of the Sun into a new zodiac sign</term> are determined in order, beginning with **Ghora** [the Terrible]. **2**

The transit named **Dhwakshi** [the Crow-like] <gloss>ॅ</gloss> affects the merchant class, while <gloss>18</gloss> others bring joy to men. The **Ghora** [the Terrible] transit affects thieves and caravans. The transit named **Mandakini** [the Gentle] affects kings, while **Mandakhya** [the Slow] affects the exalted **Brahmins** <note>original: "dvija varan"; the priestly or scholarly class</note>. The **Mishra** [the Mixed] transit affects the outcasts, and the one known as **Rakshasi** [the Demonic] affects the nature of all people. **3**

If the transit occurs in the morning, it affects the **King** and the **Brahmin**. If it occurs at midday, it affects the **Merchants**. In the afternoon, it affects the **Laborers** <note>original: "shudra"; the working class</note>. At sunset and during the evening twilight, it affects the **Hungry Ghosts** <term>Pishacha</term>. At midnight, it affects the **Night-stalkers**. **4**

During the late night, it affects **Actors** and performers. At the time of dawn <term>Pratyusha</term>, it affects the **Cattle-herders**. A transit of the Sun at the time of the evening twilight destroys or negates all auspicious signs and omens. **5**

The **Moon** <note>original: "Soma"</note> in the month of **Jyeshtha** <note>the lunar month falling in May–June</note> and **Ashadha** <note>June–July</note> is considered "middle," while in **Vaishakha** <note>April–May</note> it is the "additional" month that brings **abundance** <note>original: "subhiktaka"; suggesting a period of plenty or good harvest</note>. **2** 
When two new moons occur within a single solar month, the second one is known as the **intercalary month** <term>Adhimasa; an extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar year</term>. A thirteenth month is indeed heard of in the **Sacred Scriptures** <note>original: "Shruti"</note>, but a fourteenth month is never seen. **3**

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the Sun’s transit (Sankranti). In a healthy man, for as long as his eye pulses <note>original: "spanditi"; referring to the duration of a natural blink or a subtle twitch of the eyelid</note>,
<margin>32</margin> that blink <gloss>3</gloss> is divided into thirty parts; one such part
<margin>3</margin> is declared to be a **Tatpara** <term>Tatpara; a traditional unit of time in ancient Indian mathematics, roughly 1/30th of a blink</term>. **18** Then, a portion of that
is defined as a <gloss>1</gloss> **Truti** <term>Truti; one of the smallest units of time in Vedic cosmology, often calculated as approximately 1/32,400th of a second</term>. By
thousands of such **Trutis**, that time is known as the **Sun’s Transit** <note>original: "Ravi-sankrama"</note>. **18**
<gloss>2</gloss> At that precise moment, <gloss>2</gloss> the **Three Worlds** <term>Trailokyam; the physical, celestial, and subterranean realms</term>,
including all things moving and unmoving, become "liquefied" <note>original: "dravi-bhavati"; likely a metaphorical reference to the subtle state of flux or transition at the moment of cosmic alignment</note>. Even **Brahma** <note>the Creator deity</note> does
not know the exact instant; how much more so for an ordinary person? **20**

# The Sprout that Removes Obstacles

...[The calculation involves] the **Ghatis** <note>A traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> and the remaining degrees. Multiplying by the Sun's position and taking the remainder, the states of the **Moon** <note>original: "Shashinah"</note> are found through the **Zodiac** <note>original: "Kriyadya," starting with Aries</note> and the **Ninth-Divisions** <term>Navamsha<note>A fundamental 1/9th division of a zodiac sign used to calculate planetary strength</note></term>...

When the Moon is in the state of **Residing Abroad** <term>Pravasa-stha</term>, the result is **Travel** <term>Pravasah</term>. In this state, there is a **Certainty of loss** <term>Hani-nishchaya</term> and the loss of elders.

<margin>**Loss of affection** <note>original: "hasneha"</note></margin>
This is known from the state of the Moon...

<margin>**Prosperity** <note>original: "sampa"</note></margin>
One becomes a **Slayer of the King's enemies** <note>original: "raja-ari-ha"</note> in the 10th state, the **State of Play** <term>Krida-avastha</term>.

<margin>**Death** <note>original: "mrityu"</note></margin>
In the state of **Play** <note>original: "Krida"</note>, one should **Point out** <term>Vinirdishe</term> auspicious and favorable results.

In the 11th state, the **State of Sleep** <term>Supta-avastha</term>, through the influence of the Moon, one **Obtains happiness and income** <term>Saukhyam agamam avapnuyat 11</term>. 

And in the state of **Fevered** <term>Jvarite</term>, one should **Understand it as fever** <term>Jvaram vidyam</term>, as well as trembling and fear. These are the **Stable and Unstable** <term>Sthira-susthiram</term> results to be applied **Everywhere** <term>Sarvatra</term>.

When the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna<note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of an event.</note></term> is in the **Sign of the Fish** <note>original: "jhaṣarāśau" — Pisces</note>, aspected by **Jupiter** <term>Guru</term>, and auspicious planets are situated in the **Fourth House** <note>original: "hibukasthĕ" — a term derived from the Greek *hypogeion*, referring to the house of home, foundations, and the heart.</note>, the commencement of any task will surely lead to success. ॥ 34 ॥

In the **Tenth House** <note>original: "dashame"</note>, the **Eleventh House** <note>original: "ekādasha"</note>, or the **Ascendant**, if these are joined by the **Wise Ones** <note>original: "vibudhaiḥ" — referring to the benefic planets Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus</note>, or if such benefics are placed in **Trines** <term>Trikona<note>The 1st, 5th, or 9th houses, considered the most auspicious houses in a chart.</note></term>, success is indicated. However, one should avoid the **Rising of the Water-Pot** <note>original: "ghaṭodayaṃ" — the sign of Aquarius</note> and avoid the configuration known as the **Scissors of Malefics** <note>original: "pāpasya kartrīktam" — *Papa-kartari yoga*, where a house or planet is "pinched" between two malefic planets, neutralizing its positive effects.</note>. ॥ 36 ॥

When **Jupiter** <term>Jiva</term> or **Mercury** <term>Jnya</term> is in the Ascendant, or even if malefic planets are weak, and the **Midheaven** <term>Meshurana<note>original: "meṣūraṇasthe" — a Greek-derived Sanskrit term for the 10th house, representing one's public life and actions.</note></term> is occupied by benefics—or when the **Moon** <term>Chandra</term> is in the **House of Gains** <note>original: "āye" — the 11th house</note>—all initiations of **Journeys and Travels** <note>original: "yātrayānām"</note> will result in certain and absolute success. ॥ 37 ॥

Furthermore, in the absence of the **House of Death** <note>original: "nidhanamaye" — the 8th house, which should be avoided for new beginnings</note>, when the planets are auspicious and well-supported, let there be strength. Configurations for **Longevity** <note>original: "āyuṣya"</note> and those that are **Beneficial** <note>original: "hitakārāṇi"</note> have been described; these should certainly be utilized for the **Administration of Medicine** <note>original: "auṣadha"</note> or for healing. ॥ 38 ॥

It follows the previous page's discussion on the placement of benefics in trines and the midheaven.</meta>

<margin>43</margin>

If the planet representing **Fortune** <note>original: "dharma" — referring to the 9th house or its lord</note> or the **Moon** <note>original: "te" is likely a corruption of "indu" or "vidhu"</note> is placed in the **Trines** <note>original: "tilokhe" — likely a corruption of *trikone*, the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note>, or if all planets are favorable to the wife, the **Attainment of Wealth** <term>Dhanasiddhi<note>A specific success in financial or material endeavors through astrological timing.</note></term> is achieved. ॥ 39 ॥

When the **Fifth House** <note>original: "pañcaśayā" — the house of intellect and spiritual practices</note> is occupied by **Mars** <note>original: "mangale" — reconstructed from the OCR "māgnalĕ"</note> and is perfectly pure, or even if it is in the house of **Venus** <note>original: "rame" — referring to Rama or Lakshmi, often associated with Venus/beauty</note>, one achieves the **Mastery of Spirits** <term>Vetala Siddhi<note>The attainment of supernatural powers over a *Vetala*, a ghost-like being from Indian folklore that inhabits corpses. This is a common theme in medieval tantric and astrological texts.</note></term>. This is also known to occur when a planet is in the **Netherworld House** <note>original: "pātāle" — the 4th house, representing the foundations and hidden things</note> and the **Sign of the Deer** <note>original: "mṛgo" — Capricorn</note> is at the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>. ॥ 40 ॥

When the **Moon** is accompanied by a **Malefic** <note>original: "sakrure"</note>, situated in **Malefic Divisions** <term>Kruravarga<note>Subdivisions of the zodiac, like the Navamsha, ruled by harsh planets like Mars or Saturn.</note></term>, yet is strong in **Knowledge** <note>original: "bodhane" — often referring to Mercury's influence</note>, and during an **Inauspicious Combination** <term>Rishtayoga<note>A configuration typically indicating misfortune or ill-health, here repurposed for specific ritual timing.</note></term>, one should perform **Exorcism or Defensive Magic** <term>Abhichara<note>Rituals intended to harm enemies or remove supernatural influences. Paradoxically, these are often timed during otherwise "bad" astrological moments.</note></term> in the **Eighth House** <note>original: "naidhane" — the house of death and transformation</note>. ॥ 41 ॥

In the **Twelfth House** <note>original: "vyaye"</note> or the **Eighth House**, when the two **Wise Planets** <note>original: "sujñau" — likely Mercury and Jupiter</note> are aspected by others, or at the rise of the **Houses of Increase** <term>Upachaya<note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses, which represent growth and the overcoming of obstacles.</note></term>, complete success in all pleasing matters is attained. This is especially true when the **Moon** is situated in an **Upachaya** house. ॥ 42 ॥

When the **Moon** <note>original: "shītagau" — literally "the cool-rayed one"</note> is weak, yet the **Ascendant** is possessed of power, and **Saturn** <note>original: "asita"</note>, **Mars** <note>original: "bhauma"</note>, and the **Sun** <note>original: "bhāskara"</note> are present, or when the **Moon** is in the Ascendant of a wise man and **Jupiter** <term>Guru</term> is in the Ascendant, specific **Ritual Actions** <note>original: "abhikriyā"</note> should be performed. ॥ 43 ॥

If honor is found in the **House of Death** <note>original: "nidhana" — the 8th house, which governs longevity, transformation, and hidden things</note> and the **House of Piety** <note>original: "dharma" — the 9th house</note> is occupied by the "Lord of Herbs" <note>original: "oṣadhī" — a common epithet for the Moon, who nourishes plants with cool light</note>, or if **Malefics** <note>original: "pāpāḥ" — naturally "cruel" planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are situated in the **Angles** <term>Kendra<note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which act as the pillars of a horoscope.</note></term> of the 8th house, it results in the **Attainment of All Goals** <term>Sarvarthasiddhi</term>. If the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> is well-disposed and accompanied by **Benefics** <note>original: "saumya" — "gentle" planets like Jupiter or Venus</note>, then the 8th house is not auspicious for the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>. ॥ 47 ॥

In every case, in the first consideration of the **Ascendant**, the strength of the **Performer** <note>original: "kartuḥ" — the person for whom the astrological timing is being calculated</note> is paramount. When the **Cold-Rayed One** <note>original: "śītaraśmau" — the Moon</note> is endowed with strength, then all the **Lords of the Planets** <note>original: "grahendrāḥ"</note> become strong. ॥ 55 ॥

At the beginning of any undertaking, the strength spoken of by the ancient sages is the foundational power of the **Planetary System** <note>original: "graha-tantra-vīryaṃ"</note>. In this foundational power <margin>y</margin> resides the strength of proximity. ॥ 46 ॥ 

From the strength of the **Nectar-Rayed One** <note>original: "amṛta kiraṇa" — another poetic name for the Moon</note>, all planetary lords grant their strength. Indeed, these **Sky-Goers** <note>original: "khacara" — planets</note> give results—whether good or bad—within their own respective domains. For that reason, without this strength, one does not obtain the fruit...

For those planets that have risen in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, their application is thus: one must consider the results of the **Sky-goers** <note>original: "khecharaiḥ" — a poetic term for planets, literally "those who move through the sky"</note> in the **House of Wealth** <note>original: "dhane" — the 2nd house of the horoscope</note> and the **Ascendant**. The divisions such as the **Ninth-part** <term>Navamshaka<note>A 1/9th division of a zodiac sign (3°20'), used to determine the internal strength of a planet.</note></term> and the **Thirtieth-part** <term>Trimshanshaka<note>A 1/30th division of a sign (1°), used to assess specific strengths and character traits.</note></term> were declared by the sage **Garga**. Strength depends on these divisions and their respective rulers. ॥ 49 ॥

The following is the actual method for establishing the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna-sampādana</term>. Even if many **Faults** <note>original: "dosha" — astrological blemishes or unfavorable planetary combinations</note> are observed by practitioners, such a defect does not prevail if there is an accumulation of "soft strength" <note>original: "mṛdu-bala" — likely referring to the influence of "gentle" planets like Venus or a strong Moon</note>. In such a case, the fault becomes very small, like a single drop of water falling upon a vast surface.

# The Effects of Jupiter and the Conclusion of the Thirteenth Chapter

When **Jupiter** <term>Bṛhaspati</term> has set <note>Original: "astaṃ gate"; this refers to 'combustion,' a state where a planet is so close to the Sun that its light and astrological influence are traditionally thought to be weakened.</note>, it causes even a person born into a family of wise men to fall into servitude. ॥ 21 ॥ 

When **Jupiter** <term>Guru</term> is situated in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> in the sign of **Sagittarius** <note>Original: "dhanuṣi"</note>, or in the seventh house, or when the **Moon** <note>Original: "vidhau"</note> is in the sixth or eleventh house from the **Sun** <note>Original: "tigmāṃśo"; literally 'the one with hot rays'</note>, the person born undoubtedly achieves victory. Such a person becomes a leader among the **twice-born** <term>Brahmins<note>Original: "dvijai"</note></term>. ॥ 22 ॥

<margin>When the lords of the **Ascendant** <term>Vilagna</term>, the **Birth Moon**, and the **Tenth House** <note>Original: "daśa"; referring to the Dashama-bhava, the house of profession and public standing</note> are strong, and when the **Sun** <note>Original: "mārttaṇḍa"</note> and **Saturn** <note>Original: "dhānī-tanaya"; literally 'the son of the Sun'</note> are powerful; and when **Jupiter**, the **Moon**, and **Venus** <note>Original: "śukraḥ"</note> are born in radiant degrees <term>Amsha</term>, then the **consecration** <term>Abhiṣeka<note>The ritual bathing and coronation of a monarch.</note></term> of the **Lords of the Earth** <term>Kings</term> is desired. ॥ 1 ॥ 

When planets are in their own **Trines** <term>Trikona</term> or their own houses, they bestow wealth and fame. However, if they are set <note>combust</note>, in an enemy's house, or **debilitated** <note>Original: "nīcagā"; the position where a planet is at its weakest</note>, they bring sorrow to the sons of kings. ॥ 2 ॥ 

At the time of birth, if the Moon is in an **Increasing House** <term>Upachaya</term>, in a **Fixed Sign** <term>Sthira Lagna</term>, or rising with its head first...</margin>

# The Influence of Malefic Planets and House Placements

If the planet is viewed with a **cruel glance** <term>malefic aspect</term> <note>Original: "krūra dṛṣṭi"; in Vedic astrology, an "aspect" is the influence one planet exerts over another house or planet by its "sight."</note> and is devoid of proper honors, its auspicious power is lost. ॥ 3 ॥ 

When the **auspicious star** <term>Tārikā Śrī</term> is fallen or if a cruel planet is situated on that day in the **Twelfth House** <term>Vyaya</term>, the **Trines** <term>Trikoṇa</term>, or the **Angles** <term>Kaṇṭaka</term> <note>These are specific houses in a birth chart: the 12th (loss), the 1st/5th/9th (fortune), and the 1st/4th/7th/10th (pillars of life).</note>, even a person well-protected by the virtuous suffers loss. ॥ 4 ॥

If **malefic planets** <term>krūra</term> are situated in the **House of Wealth** <term>Dhana-bhava</term> or the **House of Gains** <term>Aya-bhava</term>, and in the **House of Death** <term>Nidhana</term>, they cause disease. 

When such planets reside in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, the **Seventh House** <term>Dyūna</term>, or the **House of Children** <term>Putra-bhava</term>, the individual is deprived of happiness <term>saukhya hīnaḥ</term>. 

The person becomes **lethargic** <term>nirudyogaḥ</term>, devoid of auspicious traits, and if these planets are in the **Ninth House** <term>Dharma</term>, the man becomes a wanderer without a home. However, if they are placed in the **House of Gains** <term>Aya</term> or the **House of Enemies** <term>Śatru</term>, he may still possess wealth and happiness. ॥ 5 ॥

Those planets residing in the **House of Death** <term>Nidhana</term> or the **House of Enemies** <term>Ripu</term> <margin>Magha <note>Original: "magha"; likely referring to the Nakshatra (lunar mansion) Magha, which is associated with ancestral power but can be harsh.</note></margin> that are also seen by **sinful planets** <term>pāpa dṛṣṭe</term> cause the person to approach the God of Death <note>Original: "yaman"; referring to Yama, the lord of the afterlife.</note>. 

Benefic planets situated in the **Twelfth** <term>Vyaya</term>, **Eighth** <term>Mṛti</term>, or **Sixth** <term>Ripu</term> houses do not yield their desired good results <term>sat-phala</term>. One whose planets are placed in such a way in the birth chart becomes like a woman <note>Original: "vanitāyate"; this likely suggests becoming subservient, delicate, or losing traditional masculine status in a historical social context.</note> in all the worlds. ॥ 6 ॥

<summary>This page details the negative impacts of malefic planets when placed in sensitive houses like the 1st, 7th, and 8th, while noting that certain placements in the 11th or 6th houses can still grant wealth despite other hardships.</summary>

<keywords>Malefic aspect, Ascendant, House of Wealth, House of Death, Bhava, Nakshatra Magha, Vedic astrology, Shripati</keywords>

If at the time of **consecration** <term>niṣeka</term> <note>In this context, it refers to the ritual anointment or the "seeding" of the royal reign.</note>, Jupiter <term>surarāja-mantrī</term> <note>Literally "the counselor of the King of the Gods."</note> is placed in the Ninth House or the **Ascendant** <term>lagna</term>, or if Venus <term>śukra</term> is in the **Trines** <term>trikoṇa</term>, and Mercury <term>sasīja</term> <note>Literally "the moon-born."</note> is situated in the house of an enemy, the king rejoices with long-lasting **prosperity** <term>lakṣmī</term>. <margin>ba</margin> ॥ 7 ॥

When Saturn <term>śani</term> is situated in the **Third House** <term>tri</term> <note>The house of courage and siblings.</note> or the **House of Gains** <term>lābha</term>, or when the Sun is in Pisces or Aquarius... <note>Text here is partially obscured by water damage.</note> ॥ 8 ॥ For the one in whose horoscope such an alignment occurs at the time of anointment, he shall surely attain a very long life.

This should also be understood regarding the enemy's army: even if [the planet] is in the **Fourth House** <term>hibuka</term> <note>The term 'hibuka' is a Sanskritization of the Greek astrological term 'hupogeion' (underground), representing the lowest point of the celestial circle or the Fourth House.</note>, victory is nonetheless certain for the seeker. Whichever zodiac sign is **Rising by the Head** <term>śīrṣodaya</term> <note>In Indian astrology, certain signs like Leo, Virgo, and Scorpio are said to rise "head-first," which is considered auspicious for the success of an undertaking.</note> brings victory; the color and nature of that rising sign should be considered accordingly. ॥ 4 ॥

A **Fixed Rising Sign** <term>sthira-udaya</term> <note>Signs like Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius, which signify stability and can be unfavorable for those needing to move quickly in war.</note> is thought to be unfavorable [for a march]; however, the result must be pronounced based on whether it is aspected by good or bad planets. ॥ 5 ॥

If a **Dual-natured Sign** <term>dvimūrti-rāśi</term> <note>Commonly known as 'Mutable' signs (Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces), which share qualities of both fixed and movable signs.</note> is rising and is conjoined with or aspected by **Malefic Planets** <term>krūra-graha</term> <note>Literally "cruel planets," usually referring to Mars, Saturn, or the Sun in certain contexts.</note>, then even if he sets out for battle at that time, he will not <margin>not</margin> return, being overwhelmed by the strength of the enemy. ॥ 6 ॥

If one hears a pleasing sound or sees a desired object, or if a favorable wind blows, then victory in that direction is yours; so it should be understood. ॥ 7 ॥

When the directions are pure and the wind is favorable, one should set out. Traveling during the day or night [without regarding these rules] leads to doubt and danger to one's life; thus, the various faults of "piercing the directions" <note>Referring to the *Vadha-vedha* defects, which are astrological obstructions that "pierce" the success of a journey.</note> are many. ॥ 16 ॥ 

On **Friday** and **Sunday**, one should not travel to the **West** <note>original: "vāruṇa-diśam" — the direction of the god Varuna.</note>. 
On **Wednesday** and **Tuesday**, one should not travel to the **North**. 
On **Saturday** and **Monday**, one should not travel to the **East**.

If they return at all, they return only under the protection of a royal umbrella <note>This implies they will only return if they are of high status or have extreme divine protection, or perhaps it refers to the shadow of a literal umbrella as a minor ritual remedy.</note>. ॥ 17 ॥ 

In whichever direction the planet **Venus** <term>śukra</term> rises, or where it moves, or where it stays—that direction is said by the sages to be "Facing Venus" <note>original: "sanmukhatva"</note>. One should exert great effort to avoid traveling in that direction when Venus is rising. ॥ 18 ॥ 

Even for a small matter, one should not travel [when facing] Venus...

<meta>This page continues the technical rules for astrological travel (yātrā), focusing specifically on the position of the planet Venus (Shukra) and the Regents of the Directions. It moves from general weekday taboos to the "Forehead of Venus" (Shukra-mukha) and the specific houses occupied by planets in the traveler’s chart.</meta>

In travel, success is certainly attained through these rules. ॥ One may travel as one wishes, except for where **Venus** <term>śukra</term> <note>The planet Venus is considered the guide of the demons and a crucial indicator for the success of any journey.</note> has set <note>original: "asta gata" — referring to heliacal setting or combustion, when a planet is too close to the sun to be seen.</note>; a person desiring victory should not travel in that direction. ॥ 19 ॥ 

When the path is blocked by the eye <note>Refers to the "Eye of Venus," a complex astrological calculation regarding the planet's visibility and position.</note> or when one moves against the natural order <note>original: "prati-loma" — against the hair or against the grain.</note> while the planet is stationed there or has gone elsewhere, a king <gloss>not</gloss> standing in the right alignment will find his mouth <note>His power or command.</note> obstructed and will fall under the singular control of his enemies. ॥ 20 ॥ 

Even in such circumstances, if the planet is moving in a favorable way, a traveler may proceed. For a king who has set out, even the weapons of the **Lord Upendra** <note>A name for Vishnu, particularly in his Vamana avatar; here it implies that even divine weapons require planetary support to work.</note> are only effective if the **Moon** <term>vidhu</term> is in a strong and capable position. ॥ 21 ॥ 

The **Lords of the Directions** are the **Sun** <term>ravi</term>, **Venus** <term>śukra</term>, **Mars** <term>bhauma</term>, **Rahu** <term>tamas</term> <note>The ascending node of the moon, often called the "darkness."</note>, **Saturn** <term>yama</term> <note>Associated here with the God of Death.</note>, the **Moon** <term>indu</term>, **Mercury** <term>jña</term>, and **Jupiter** <term>surāguru</term> <note>Literally "the Teacher of the Gods."</note>. When the Lord of the Direction is located "on the forehead" <note>original: "lalāṭage" — This refers to the planet being directly in front of the traveler, which is considered a major obstacle to success.</note>, one should certainly not go, not even a tiny insect. ॥ 22 ॥ 

If **Mars** <term>bhūmija</term> <note>Literally "Son of the Earth."</note> is situated in the **Ascendant** <term>lagna</term>, the house of loss, the eighth house, the house of children, or the house of action <term>karma</term>; or if **Rahu** is present, it leads to the destruction of wealth. If the **Son of the Sun** <term>ravi-sute</term> <note>A common epithet for Saturn (Shani).</note> is present, it brings misery. If the **Moon** <term>vidhu</term>, **Mercury** <term>jña</term>, or the **Teacher of the Gods** <term>surācārya</term> [are poorly placed], they result in sickness...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological travel (yātrā), shifting from the birth chart’s influence to the specific planetary strengths and house placements at the moment of the journey's start (the "Departure Ascendant"). It maintains the numbered sequence of verses, providing rules for which planets support or hinder success.</meta>

At the moment of departure, for the one who journeys <note>original: "yāna"</note>, the **Ascendant** <term>lagna</term> is the primary factor to be considered. ॥ 27 ॥

The physical self <note>original: "mūrti" — referring to the first house of the horoscope, representing the body of the traveler</note>, whether it is endowed with strength or not, must be analyzed. One must calculate the state of enemies <note>the 6th house</note>, the lifespan <note>original: "āyu" — the 8th house</note>, and the business or trade <note>original: "vyāpāra" — the 10th house</note> to be undertaken. <gloss>4</gloss> Indeed, the conditions of all twelve houses of the **Ascendant** <term>lagna</term> are to be contemplated for the traveler’s success. ॥ 28 ॥

Cruel or malefic planets <term>krūra-graha</term> destroy the success of one's business or journey. Specifically, **Saturn** <note>original: "sūryya-ja" — literally "born of the Sun," a common epithet for Saturn</note> is cited as a destroyer in this context. Benefic planets <term>saumya</term> <note>Literally "gentle" planets, such as Jupiter or Mercury.</note> provide stability, but if **Venus** <term>śukra</term> is setting <note>original: "asta" — also known as "combust," where a planet is too close to the Sun to be visible and thus loses its power</note>, it leads to the death or failure of the leaders of the mission. ॥ 29 ॥

<margin>Ya</margin> Malefic planets may be auspicious in certain placements, except when **Saturn** <term>shani</term> has gone into the **tenth house** <term>daśama-bhava</term> <note>The house governing public action and the height of the sky.</note> for the one desiring to depart. One must determine the state of all houses starting from the physical self <note>the 1st house</note>; benefic planets are favorable in these positions, provided they are not setting or situated in the house of children <note>the 5th house</note>. ॥ 30 ॥

Planets that have set <note>combust</note>, those defeated in planetary war <term>graha-yuddha</term> <note>A calculation where two planets are in the same degree, and one is deemed the "loser" based on its celestial latitude or brightness.</note>, and those situated in their sign of **debilitation** <term>nīca</term> <note>The specific zodiac sign where a planet's influence is at its weakest</note> are considered powerless. Even if such planets are present in the **Ascendant** <term>lagna</term> of departure, they truly yield no fruit for the traveler. ॥ 32 ॥

<summary>This page outlines the importance of the twelve astrological houses during the moment of departure, warning that malefic planets—particularly a combust Venus or Saturn in the tenth house—can ruin the success of a journey.</summary>
<keywords>Ascendant (Lagna), Saturn (Shani), Venus (Shukra), combust (asta), debilitation (nica), planetary war (graha-yuddha), travel astrology (yatra)</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological travel (yātrā), moving from the general influence of the Ascendant to the supreme importance of specific planetary combinations (yogas) and the timing of the journey. It emphasizes that the specific "chemistry" of the stars can override their individual natures.</meta>

Just as poison acts like nectar through a specific combination <term>yoga</term>, and even honey acts like poison when mixed with clarified butter <note>In Ayurveda, equal parts honey and ghee are considered toxic.</note>; so too, the planets <term>khecara</term> <note>original: "khecarāḥ" — literally "those who move in the sky."</note>, discarding their own natural fruits, provide a result born specifically from their combination <term>yoga</term>. ॥ ३३ ॥

Indeed, the manifestation of results for men does not occur simply by their own will. It arises from the power of the auspicious moment <term>muhūrtta</term> <note>A unit of time approximately 48 minutes long, chosen for its favorable alignment.</note>, the influence of omens <term>śakuna</term> <note>Signs or portents, often interpreted from the behavior of birds or animals.</note> such as birds, and even the touch of people <note>referring to chance encounters at the start of a journey</note>, through which the strength of the moment is felt. ॥ ३४ ॥
<margin>Combination</margin>

Regarding the lunar day <term>tithi</term>, the lunar mansion <term>nakṣatra</term>, the half-day <term>karaṇa</term>, the weekday <term>vāra</term>, the astrological combination <term>yoga</term>, and the rising sign <term>vilagna</term>; if these are malefic <term>pāpa</term> during the beloved journeys of kings, they do not succeed; instead, they return from there <note>original: "nivṛtyate" — to turn back or retreat</note> in distress. ॥ ३५ ॥

When Saturn <note>original: "manda"</note> and the Sun <note>original: "bhāskara"</note> are placed in certain positions <note>The text likely refers to the "body" or 1st house</note>, the king who has gone to the battlefield will find himself running back to his palace. ॥ ३६ ॥

When Saturn <note>original: "yama"</note>, Mercury <note>original: "jña"</note>, Venus <note>original: "śukra"</note>, and Mars <note>original: "mahasuta"</note>—these three <note>though four are named, the text mentions "triṣu" or three types of placements</note>—are situated in the house of birth, the eighth house <note>the house of death/obstacles</note>, or the sixth house <note>the house of enemies</note>, the army of the enemy dissolves at the front of the battlefield as easily as... <note>The text ends mid-comparison, likely comparing the dissolution to something light or melting.</note>

<summary>This page explains how the "yoga" (combination) of planets can change their fundamental nature, much like chemical reactions, and stresses that a king's success in battle depends more on the timing (muhūrtta) and omens than on personal will.</summary>

When a King sets out under a configuration <term>yoga</term> where Jupiter <term>guru</term> is in the Ascendant <term>lagna</term> and Venus <term>sita</term> is also strongly positioned, he quickly destroys his enemies, just as Indra <note>original: "Divaspati," the Lord of the Heavens.</note> destroys the demons. ॥ ४५ ॥

A marriage, an auspicious festival, the rise of prosperity, or a woman who has recently given birth <term>sūtikā</term>—these are peaceful signs.

|| The duty <term>dharma</term> <note>original: *dharma*; here referring to the socio-religious obligations of a specific life stage.</note> of a householder is well-known; there is none that stands before it. Since [the stability of society] rests upon it, and likewise upon the woman, [I shall speak of] the Moon <note>original: *śīta-raśmi*, literally "the cool-rayed one," a poetic name for the Moon.</note>... || Due to the influence of her body and the Ascendant <term>lagna</term> <note>The specific zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of an event.</note>, and indeed by the power of the Moon, I shall now declare the purification of the Ascendant for the sake of [choosing] a new bride. **1** For the one whose... the song of the Kinnara <note>A celestial musician in Hindu mythology.</note>, at the time of sunrise, a Garuda <note>A mythical eagle-like deity; here referring to an auspicious mark or planetary combination.</note> on the palm of the hand is praised. Jupiter <term>jīva</term> <note>original: *jīva*, the planet Jupiter, considered the most auspicious planet in Vedic astrology.</note> in a human sign, or situated in Sagittarius... in a leap month <term>adhika-māsa</term> <note>An extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar year; usually considered inauspicious for major life events.</note>, or during the three-day conjunction, [the rite] should be performed. **2** ||

One should not [perform these rites] in the four months starting with Ashadha <note>The beginning of *Cāturmāsya*, a four-month period of the rainy season when many auspicious ceremonies are paused.</note>, nor in the month known as Madhu <note>Another name for the spring month of Chaitra.</note>. It should certainly not be done when Venus <note>original: *bhārgava*, a name for the planet Venus.</note> or Jupiter have reached old age <term>vṛddhatva</term> or are in their childhood <term>bālabhāva</term>. **3** When Venus has risen in the east, it is a "child" <note>An astrological state where a planet is too close to the Sun or has just risen, making its influence weak or "immature."</note> for ten days, and after that, it is "old" for five days. Following the teachings of Vashistha <margin>4</margin> and others, Jupiter's five-day periods of childhood and old age in the east or west should also be avoided. **4** In the birth month, under the birth star of the leaders [the couple], and on the very day of birth...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Muhurta (electing auspicious times). It transitions from general prohibitions (like the leap month mentioned on the previous page) to specific natural portents and the construction of an astrological diagram (Cakra) used to calculate planetary "piercings" (Vedha) that might nullify an auspicious date.</meta>

Earthquakes, portentous thunders <note>original: *nirghāta*; sudden loud sounds from a clear sky, considered ill omens</note>, evil sights, comets, and the leap month <term>adhika-māsa</term> <note>An extra month added to the lunar calendar to maintain alignment with the solar seasons.</note>—these should be known as times to be avoided for travel, marriage, the sacred thread ceremony <term>vratabandha</term>, and the first hair-cutting ceremony <term>cūḍā</term>. **5**

In the case of a first-born daughter or son, one should not perform any auspicious ceremony during the month of Jyeshtha <note>The lunar month usually falling in May–June.</note>. **5** <note>The scribe repeats the verse number 5 here.</note>

When the Moon <note>original: *tuṣāra-raśmi*, literally "the cool-rayed one"</note> is conjoined with the Sages <note>likely referring to the Saptarishi or specific planetary degrees</note>, or when Venus <term>śukra</term> is in certain positions, it brings about misfortune or widowhood. However, if Jupiter <term>ijya</term> casts its gaze upon it, it bestows auspiciousness. **6**

Six vertical lines should be drawn, and similarly, two lines should be placed horizontally at the corners to form a diagram <term>cakra</term>. The station of Fire <note>the Krittika nakshatra</note> is placed in one corner, and in the second corner, the solar transit <term>saṅkrānti</term> <note>The moment the Sun moves from one zodiac sign to another.</note> is marked, extending toward the Abhijit nakshatra. **7**

The portion of Abhijit <note>A "hidden" 28th lunar mansion used in specific calculations</note> is said to consist of the last quarter of the mansion of the Universal Gods <note>the Uttara Ashadha nakshatra</note> and the first four minutes <term>nāḍya</term> <note>original: *nāḍya*; a unit of time approximately 24 minutes long.</note> of the mansion of Vishnu <note>the Shravana nakshatra</note>. In this chart, one must observe the "piercing" <term>vedha</term> <note>A technical astrological term where planets on the same line of a diagram obstruct or harm the influence of a star.</note> between the planets and the star Rohini. **8**

In that diagram, if a malefic planet creates a "piercing" through its gaze or position, it results in a downfall. **9** The twelfth star to the north, the Son of the Earth <note>original: *avani-sūnu*; the planet Mars</note> in the third, and Jupiter <term>guru</term>... the sixth and the eighth positions are destroyed by the Son of the Sun <note>original: *arkaja*; the planet Saturn</note>.

<margin>54</margin>

Wise men should understand that when planets are situated on a single line in this diagram, it determines the causes of auspiciousness or harm for men.

<meta>This page continues the technical discourse on Muhurta (auspicious timing) for religious ceremonies, specifically marriage. It follows the previous discussion on calculating planetary "piercings" and now introduces the concept of *Latta* (planetary "kicks") and the duration of ill-omens before a time becomes pure again for rituals.</meta>

...[struck] by the planetary "kick" <term>latta</term>. <note>original: *latta*; a specific astrological affliction where a planet "kicks" or affects a lunar mansion (nakshatra) at a certain distance.</note> Afterward, seven or nine <note>referring to specific time units or counts of nakshatras</note> **10**

When Rahu <note>the north node of the moon, often called the "shadow planet"</note> or Venus <note>original: *sita*</note> are in the fifth or twentieth positions, they should be avoided. The Moon <note>original: *uḍupa*</note> strikes the person’s form, but no other planet does so. **10**

A time is considered inauspicious <note>original: *naiṣṭaṃ*</note> when it is joined with malefic planets <term>krūra</term> <note>planets like Saturn, Mars, or the Sun</note>, when a planet moves toward a malefic, when it is aspected by the Sun, or when it is pierced by a malefic. Furthermore, if the time is corrupted by portents <term>utpāta</term> occurring in the sky or atmosphere, or if it is affected by a malefic planetary "kick" <term>latta</term>— **11**

<margin>pro[ceeding] <unclear>k</unclear></margin> <note>The marginal note "pra ka" likely refers to *prakaraṇa*, indicating a specific section or chapter reference.</note>

—or if there is a "war" between malefic planets <note>a planetary war or *yuddha* occurs when two planets are at very close longitudinal proximity</note>, or it is corrupted by the three types of portents, or falls within the inauspicious time of *Vishti* <note>a specific division of the day considered unlucky for starting new works</note>, no auspicious ceremonies <term>maṅgaleṣu</term> should be performed. Only after the disturbances of meteors <note>original: *ulkā*</note> or thunderbolts <note>original: *aśani*</note> have passed, and the experts deem the time to be clear <note>original: *nirmala*</note>, should one proceed for an auspicious purpose. **12**

In the ritual of "taking the hand" <term>pāṇigrahe</term> <note>referring to the marriage ceremony</note>, the relationship of the sixth and eighth houses <note>original: *ṣaṣṭhāṣṭake*; a configuration known as Shashtashtaka, usually considered highly unfavorable as it signifies enmity or death</note> leads to death or loss of children. A relationship of the ninth and fifth houses <note>original: *nava-pañcame*</note> results in friendship, while the second and twelfth positions <note>original: *dhana-dvādaśake*</note> affect wealth and expenditure. However, love may still exist between the couple if the lords of their signs are the same. **13**

If the lord of the bride’s sign <note>original: *bhuvanapati*</note> is a friend of the groom's, or if they share the same planetary ruler, then even with other afflictions, the marriage can be auspicious. This is especially true if there is purity of the stars <term>tārā-viśuddhau</term> <note>a calculation based on the couple's birth stars to ensure compatibility</note> for the groom. **14**

### The Assembly of Channels and Vital Breath

...In the state of exhalation <term>recaka</term> <note>the specific yogic practice of controlled breathing out</note>, the assembly of channels <term>nāḍī</term> <note>subtle conduits in the body through which life-force flows</note> is established. **54**

The channels flow through the body's currents without remainder. They pulsate from other channels and are thus produced in the shape of a serpent <note>original: *sarpa-ākāra*; a common description for the winding path of subtle energy or Kundalini</note>. Among them, the multitude of channels is governed by the single vital breath <term>prāṇa</term> <note>the primary life-force</note>, which resides in the intellect of the wise. **55**

By mastering the breath, one's self becomes like a jewel. [Through this mastery], the blood is purified. 

<margin>Varpa</margin> <note>Original: *Varpa*; this may be a proper name or a specific technical term for a type of energy</note> Know that by binding the collection of rays, one attains the higher worlds. <margin>58</margin> <note>The number 58 is written in the margin, corresponding to the page number</note> **56**

### Rules of Form and Physical Signs

Through the rules of bodily form <term>mūrtividhāna</term> <note>the science of interpreting physical features to predict character or destiny</note>, one finds wealth and prosperity. Good character, virtue, and conduct [are indicated]. However, if [malefic signs] are present in the body, it leads to the loss of wealth and the wasting of the frame, whether in men or in women <term>yoṣitāṃ</term>. **57**

A memory of the vital breath brings a wealth of resources. It is certain that she will have a daughter and a son.
