# Predictions for Women: Wealth and Widowhood

Renuka <note>likely a reference to a specific astrological authority or a specific planetary yoga</note>, from this [alignment] comes widowhood <term>vaidhavyā</term> <note>the state of losing one's husband, a central concern in historical Indian horoscopy</note> and poverty <term>daridratā</term>. Conversely, one may possess great wealth <term>sudhanatā</term> and a good nature. She may be abandoned by her children or her husband; she might rise to prominence or fall into bad conduct <term>kucaritā</term>. She may experience conflict with her own brothers or opposition to her husband. She may take pleasure in deceit or be a woman who celebrates the birth and success of her sons. **18-19**

---

### The Influence of Benefic Planets

Through a benefic planet <term>saumya</term> <note>original: *saumya*; refers to "gentle" or auspicious planets like Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, or the waxing Moon</note>, she is devoted to the highest path and possesses good character <term>sucaritrā</term>. She may be a learned woman, yet widowhood is possible [under specific conditions]. She may be endowed with sons, yet be conquered by others or hostile toward her own relatives. She might be deceptive <term>māyāvinī</term>, born of a [low] lineage, living in poverty, and intent on excessive spending, depending on the position of the Ascendant <term>lagna</term> <note>the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note>. **20**

---

### Signs of a Virtuous Woman

[She becomes] a sharer in her master’s wealth, joyful and endowed with possessions. She possesses self-knowledge, is beautiful, and is beloved. She is skilled in song <note>original: *gīta*</note>, and finds delight in the excellence of her life-force. Successful in her aims <term>siddhārtha</term> and endowed with many [virtues], she is a leader among her peers. Such a woman is known as a virtuous lady <term>sunārī</term>.

**Income and Expenditure**
<note>original: *āya vyaya*; in traditional Indian architecture (Vastu Shastra), these are mathematical formulas used to determine the dimensions of a building to ensure it remains in a state of "profit" or positive energy rather than "loss" (expenditure)</note>

Having properly understood the sequence of the stars <term>taravali</term> and the state of bliss, one should determine that which increases lifespan, wealth, health, and fame, and which provides wisdom to the people. **1** 

The eight types of orientations <note>original: *pāyāṣṭakam*; referring to the *Ashtaya* or eight mathematical formulas based on the remainder of calculations</note> are declared as:
1. The Banner <term>Dhvaja</term>
2. Smoke <term>Dhumra</term>
3. The Lion <term>Simha</term>
4. The Dog <term>Shvana</term>
5. The Bull <term>Vrisha</term>
6. The Donkey <term>Khara</term>
7. The Elephant <term>Gaja</term>
8. The Crow <term>Shunya</term> <note>the text lists "Shunya" (void), which is often associated with the Crow/Bhanka in other systems</note> **2**

The expansion of the house should be according to the "Income" <term>Aya</term> formula; when the wealth is in harmony with the Lord of Wealth <term>Yakshesha</term>, the house becomes auspicious. Among the Banner and the others, those that are situated in their own proper directions are highly praised. The Banner <term>Dhvaja</term> is considered the best for all types of construction. **3**

The Banner is situated in the East, the Lion in the South, the Bull in the West, and the Elephant in the North. Others are placed in the intermediate directions. If the Banner is placed in the middle of a hero’s dwelling, it brings... <note>text becomes fragmentary</note> ...expenditure and other results. **4**

Among these eight, the results are... [broken text] ...not for a man...

**The Ten Types of Halls**

The names of the houses are:
1. **Dhruva** (The Constant)
2. **Dhanya** (The Fortunate)
3. **Jaya** (The Victorious)
4. **Nanda** (The Joyful)
5. **Svara** (The Harmonious)
6. **Kanta** (The Beloved)
7. **Manorama** (The Pleasing)
8. **Sukantha** (The Sweet-voiced)
9. **Vipulaksha** (The Large-eyed)
10. **Vijaya** (The Triumphant) **7-8**

One should know these as the individual hall-arrangements <term>pratyeka-shālā</term>. Now I shall describe the method for their expansion and permutation <term>prastara</term>. **8** 

**The Method of Permutations**

Place a "light" mark <term>laghu</term> <note>symbolizing a short unit or a specific architectural feature</note> below a "heavy" mark <term>guru</term> <note>symbolizing a long or double unit</note>. Above these two, and proceeding to the west, one should repeat the heavy marks. This is the rule for "finding the lost" <term>nashta-labdha-vidhi</term> <note>a mathematical method used to reconstruct a specific pattern within a series of possible permutations</note>. **9**

A veranda <term>alinda</term> with seven openings should be placed clockwise <term>pradakshinam</term>, depending on the placement of the light units. If the heavy units are placed at the beginning, according to the lord Indra, there appear sixteen types of *Kuneras* <note>specific auspicious structural patterns or "storeys"</note>. **10**

<summary>This page details the mathematical formulas for calculating the 'expenditure' of a building and lists the ten traditional names for house halls (Shalas). It also introduces the Nashtalabdha method, a combinatoric system used to determine structural variations and the placement of verandas.</summary>
<keywords>Vastu Shastra, architectural mathematics, Dhruva, Shala types, Alinda, Nashtalabdha, house-building rituals</keywords>

--- Page 126 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions of the Vastu Shastra (architectural science). While the previous page categorized the ten types of auspicious halls, this section shifts focus to the professional responsibility of the architect, the prohibited months for construction, and the astrological alignment of a house’s orientation based on the Sun’s position in the zodiac.</meta>

In the hand of the architect <gloss>indeed</gloss>, one should construct the house and the city. In that place, a structure should never be allowed to fall into ruin or be built without clear proportions. Therefore, this is stated with great care. **11**

Construction of residences is forbidden in the months of Ashadha <note>June–July</note>, Chaitra <note>March–April</note>, and Ashwayuja <note>September–October</note>. According to the opinion of the teacher Yogeshvaracharya <note>A traditional authority on astrology and architecture</note>, building during these times is considered inauspicious. **12**

<margin>Trai</margin> In the [specified months], there is the loss of livestock; <insert>attaining wealth and its enjoyment</insert> <margin>Ko</margin> war and conflict; <gloss>for whom</gloss> fame and money; for the increase of prosperity <margin>Ma</margin> and <gloss>how</gloss> the arrival of Lakshmi <note>The Goddess of Wealth and Fortune</note> <gloss>how</gloss> is attained in the house. This sequence for the months beginning with Chaitra was spoken by the Sages and taught in the <gloss>near</gloss> scriptures of architecture <term>Vastu Shastra</term>. **13**

When the Sun is in the signs of the Crab <term>Karka</term> <note>Cancer</note>, the Sea-Monster <term>Nakra</term> <note>Capricorn</note>, the Lion <term>Hari</term> <note>Leo</note>, or the Water-Pot <term>Kumbha</term> <note>Aquarius</note>, one should build houses facing East or West. When the Sun is in the Scales <term>Tula</term> <note>Libra</note>, the Ram <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note>, the Bull <term>Vrisha</term> <note>Taurus</note>, or the Scorpion <term>Vrischika</term> <note>Scorpio</note>, one should make the houses face South or North. **14**

Otherwise, if a person acts out of foolishness, they will suffer from disease, grief, and the destruction of wealth. When the Sun is in the Fish <term>Meena</term> <note>Pisces</note>, the Bow <term>Chapa</term> <note>Sagittarius</note>, the Twins <term>Mithuna</term> <note>Gemini</note>, or the Maiden <term>Angana</term> <note>Virgo</note>, one should carry out the construction of cities and houses. **15** [This leads to] the successful completion of work and happiness.

<summary>This page outlines the astrological timing and orientation for building houses and cities, warning that construction during prohibited months or with incorrect directional alignment leads to misfortune and poverty.</summary>
<keywords>Vastu Shastra, Yogeshvaracharya, zodiac signs, architectural orientation, auspicious months, house construction, Lakshmi, technical astrology</keywords>

--- Page 127 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions of the Vastu Shastra (architectural science), specifically focusing on the astrological timing (Muhurta) for construction. Following the discussion on auspicious months and orientation, the text now details specific planetary positions and omens that determine the success, failure, or longevity of a building.</meta>

If one observes bad omens <term>ashakuna</term> <note>original: "ashakuna"; these are traditional Indian signs of impending misfortune, such as specific animal sounds or sightings</note> or if it is known that animals are being struck or beaten at that time, a wise architect should not declare that moment suitable for beginning construction. <margin>82</margin> **16**

When the rising sign <term>Lagna</term> <note>The astrological sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of commencement</note> is a fixed sign and gentle <term>saumya</term> <note>Benefic planets, traditionally Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, and the waxing Moon</note> planets aspect it, or if strong benefic planets are situated in the tenth house <note>The house of professional success and karma</note>, then the construction of the building will not meet with failure. **17**

If harsh <term>papa</term> <note>Malefic planets, traditionally Mars, Saturn, Rahu, or Ketu</note> planets are situated in the eighth house <note>The house of death and transformation</note>, the building becomes like a house of smoke <note>An idiom suggesting a state of ruin or a haunted, empty space</note>. It is said that construction at such a time brings death to the king of the land and causes the builder to perish in conflict. **18**

When the Moon <note>original: "shashanka"</note> is in its own auspicious alignment, and Venus and Jupiter <note>original: "devaguru", the teacher of the gods</note> are in the house of wealth <note>The second astrological house</note>, prosperity is granted. However, if these planets are devoid of strength, situated in their signs of debilitation, or are combust <note>original: "astamupagate"; when a planet is too close to the sun to be visible, losing its power</note>, the structure will fall into division and decay. **19**

If Jupiter is in the rising sign, Mercury <note>original: "shashija", the son of the moon</note> is in the house of setting <note>The seventh house, opposite the ascendant</note>, and Venus is in a water sign <margin>82</margin>, the wise declare that such a house will have a lifespan of a hundred years. **20**

If Mercury is in the rising sign, Jupiter is in its own sign, and the Sun <note>original: "dinakrit", the maker of the day</note> is in the house of gains <note>The eleventh house</note>, then a house of such character will certainly endure for a hundred years. <margin>82</margin> **21**

<summary>This page details the astrological requirements for a building's longevity and the omens that must be avoided. It specifies that the placement of benefic planets like Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury in key astrological houses can grant a structure a lifespan of one hundred years.</summary>

<keywords>astrology, omens, longevity, Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, fixed signs, house of wealth, building lifespan, Vastu Shastra</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of construction and personal destiny. The previous page detailed the physical signs and omens (ashakuna) that dictate the success of a building. If Mercury <note>original: "Indu-putra," literally the 'Son of the Moon'</note> is situated in the rising sign <term>Lagna</term> and Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva," meaning the life-force or soul</note> is placed in the third house, the undertaking achieves its intended purpose and secures a wealth of essence. **22**

When the Moon and Jupiter are aligned in a virtuous year, and even if Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> and other planets are in difficult positions, the structure will not face destruction. It is said that such a placement ensures the building remains standing for eighty years. **23**

If the Counselor of the Gods <note>Jupiter; original: "Devamantri"</note> is in an exalted state in the rising sign, and Mercury is in the fourth house <note>original: "Rasatala," the subterranean house or the 'bottom' of the chart</note>, and the Son of the Sun <note>Saturn; original: "Ravi-sute"</note> is positioned elsewhere, the work will possess a brilliant status and endurance. **24**

When planets are in their own signs or friendly signs within the rising chart, the Goddess of Fortune <term>Lakshmi</term> will be present and wealth will be attained. However, if the planets are in their debilitated states <note>original: "neecha," the point where a planet is at its weakest</note> or in enemy houses, one becomes penniless <term>nirdhana</term>. **25**

Even a single planet located in the house of loss <note>the 12th house; original: "vyaya"</note> or the house of desire <note>the 7th house; original: "smara"</note> can cause a man to live in the forest or depend on the hands of others, particularly if the Lord of the Year <term>Varshanatha</term> is weak in strength. **26**

He who neglects the cows, the gods, the sacred fire, and the teacher <note>Guru</note> will find himself in the service of others. He will not sustain his lineage, and his life will be consumed by the pursuit of women and food. **27** <gloss>Thus ends the Lord of Birth-Horoscopy</gloss>

# Composed in the *Jewel-Garland of Astrology*
## Chapter Nine and Ten: On the Ritual of Entry

Now, regarding the entry into a new residence. After the purification baths are completed and during the days of the northern solstice <note>original: "saumyayaneshvara dine," referring to Uttarayana, the auspicious six-month period when the sun travels north</note>, one should perform the Vastu worship <term>Vastu-archana</term> <note>rituals to appease the spirit of the site</note> and ensure the planets are at their full strength. **1**

If the entry occurs under auspicious planets, prosperity follows; however, [if poorly timed], there may be disease, famine, fear of chronic illness, and sorrow for the person. Travel or inauguration under fierce constellations or those associated with cruel rulers, such as the Star of the Branch <note>original: "Vishakha"</note>, leads to total destruction. **2**

The constellation of the Goddess of Fire <note>original: "Krittika"</note> is charming and brings good resonance; there is no doubt for those who enter under its influence. Whatever the orientation of the house <note>original: "mukha-sadana," the 'face' or entrance of the house</note>, one should act accordingly, but one must not enter through an unfavorable entrance. **3**

The Empty Days <term>Rikta Tithis</term> <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered hollow or unlucky for new beginnings</note> and Sundays are censured and should be avoided, as should inauspicious planetary combinations. The signs of the Ram <note>Aries</note>, the Crab <note>Cancer</note>, the Sea-Monster <note>Capricorn</note>, and the Scales <note>Libra</note> must be avoided for the ritual of entry <note>these are "movable" signs, which are considered too unstable for the permanent foundation of a home</note>, as should the New Moon <term>Darsha</term>. **4**

Jupiter is the projector of influence. When the seventh house is pure, and when the third, eighth, and tenth houses are favorable for the master of the house, it constitutes an auspicious placement for settling into the residence. **5**

When the trines <term>Trikona</term> <note>the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note> and cardines <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> are occupied by auspicious planets, and the "thorns" <note>malefic planets in sensitive positions</note> are absent, the outcome is beneficial. Entry during a fixed rising sign <term>Sthira Lagna</term> <note>signs like Taurus or Leo that represent permanence</note> is purifying for the Moon. **6** Meru...

<summary>This page outlines the astrological requirements for 'Griha Pravesha' (house-warming), specifying auspicious solar cycles, constellations to avoid, and the necessity of stable zodiac signs to ensure the home's longevity.</summary>
<keywords>Griha Pravesha, Vastu-archana, Uttarayana, Rikta Tithis, Trikona, Kendra, fixed signs, Krittika, Vishakha</keywords>

The expansion of the household, the prosperity of the sun-like master, and the gift of children and wives may be destroyed; life itself may be taken and the house ruined unless the ritual is performed correctly and in the proper order. **7** Regarding the Moon-sign or the Ascendant: <margin>or</margin>
<margin>not</margin> the sages have declared that the rising of one’s birth sign or birth ascendant is auspicious for this purpose; however, [entering at other times] brings no fame to the house entry. **8** When the cardinal houses <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a birth chart, considered the pillars of a horoscope</note> and the house of expenditure <note>the 12th house</note> <margin>are</margin>
<margin>purified</margin> are clear of malefic influence; when the cruel planets are situated in the sixth, third, or eleventh houses; and when Jupiter is in the ascendant or a cardinal house in a fixed sign—this is considered especially favorable for entering a home in a village. **9**

Under the constellation of the "Return of Wealth" <note>original: "Punarvasu," the 7th Nakshatra</note>, the birthing chamber <term>Sutika-griha</term> <note>a specialized room for childbirth and the first weeks of an infant's life</note> should be constructed. With the blessings of the Creator <note>original: "Viranchi," a name for Brahma</note>, the Preserver <note>Vishnu</note>, and the Sun <note>Bhaskara</note>, the entry ritual is performed for one's welfare. **10** Having honored the priests <note>Brahmins</note>, and carrying jars filled with water, milk, and clarified butter; with the sun positioned to the left; having bathed and wearing garlands and spotless garments; amidst the auspicious sounds of songs and music; and having observed the omens on the path to the door—the King enters the mansion <term>Harmyam</term>, which is beautiful with scattered flowers and especially with the ceremonial arched gateway <term>Torana</term>. **11**

Then the King should honor the priests, the gods, the architects <note>original: "Shilpajña," those knowledgeable in the arts of building and sculpting</note>, and the master of the treasury with wealth, gems, and cattle, always according to [custom]...

### **Thus ends the chapter on House Entry in the *Jewel-Garland of Astrology* <note>original: "Jyotiratnamālā"</note> composed by the astrologer Śrīpati.**

The results of obtaining or wearing new garments [based on the lunar mansions] are as follows: the acquisition of clothes, beauty, destruction by fire, the attainment of solar-like power, political wisdom, the acquisition of wealth, the loss of money, and grief. Furthermore, one may attain kingship, prosperity, success in one’s labors, and the fulfillment of all desires. For people, there is also the attainment of... <note>The text here lists the specific effects of the 27 Nakshatras or lunar mansions on new clothing</note> ...attainment of lordship, being drenched in water, passion, pleasure, desirable food, and eye disease. One may also find abundance in grain, the loss of stability, water, wealth, and jewels. These are the fruits starting from the constellation of the "Horsemen" <note>original: "Ashvini," the first of the 27 Nakshatras</note>.

**2** The gods reside there... <note>This section appears to discuss the positioning of divine energies within household objects</note> ...for safety and for the protection of cattle. In the middle sections, there is protection and guardianship. **13**

Regarding the use or construction of beds, seats, and sandals: **3** The attainment of companionship, honor from the gods and the queen, and the birth of a son shall come to pass...

A garment that is given with affection by a Brahmin from another land or by a protector is considered favorable. It is said that at other times, such a garment should not be worn. **6**

### **Now, the Consecration of the Divine Images**

During the sun's northern course <note>original: "Uttarayāna," the auspicious six-month period after the winter solstice</note>, on a day of a beneficial deity, a favorable lunar day, and a propitious moment, when the moon is waxing and the zodiac sign <term>Lagna: the ascending sign on the eastern horizon</term> is viewed by auspicious planets... **7**

When the sun is in the northern signs <note>the text likely refers to the signs from Capricorn to Gemini</note>, and the moon is in a favorable position, one should perform the ritual for the residence of the deity. The life-infusing ceremony <term>Pratishthā: the ritual that "awakens" the divine presence in a statue</term> and the offering of flowers should be performed as prescribed.

For the primary deities such as Saraswati <note>the goddess of wisdom and music</note> and others, as well as for those remaining, and for all the Guardians of the World <term>Lokapālas: deities presiding over the cardinal directions</term> as previously mentioned, the installation is said to be best performed when the zodiac signs are fixed. **3**

When Leo <note>Simha</note> is rising, the Sun should be established; in Gemini <note>Mithuna</note>, the Great Lord <note>Shiva</note> and Narayana <note>Vishnu</note>; in Aquarius <note>Ghata</note>, the Creator <note>Brahma</note>. All these gods should be placed in fixed houses <note>zodiac signs like Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, or Aquarius</note> that are highly brilliant. <margin>65</margin> **4**

Wherever the Seven Sages <term>Saptarishi: the seven stars of the Big Dipper, identified as ancient patriarchs</term> are traversing, the consecration of their images should indeed be performed. This also applies to the illustrious Vyasa <note>the traditional compiler of the Vedas</note>, Valmiki <note>the author of the Ramayana</note>, and the Jar-Born Sage <note>original: "Ghatodbhava," referring to Agastya</note>. Their installation is performed through the speech and strength of the sacred hymns. **5**

A well-performed consecration for the Sun and other deities becomes radiant and bestows security. It prevents the danger of fire, grants wealth, is enduring, and ensures that the deity resides there, providing food and prosperity for a long time. **6**

...when worldy attachments are stilled, one should certainly perform the installation of the image. This act is intended to secure children, happiness, and prosperity, and to appease any anger. **7**

When a beneficial zodiac sign is rising <note>the ascendant or 'Lagna'</note>, and is occupied by or associated with auspicious planets, it bestows strength. Having considered the power of the planetary positions, the astrologer should determine what is beneficial. This produces the desired results for the person commissioning the rite. **8**

From the qualities and fruits of the year, the lunar day <note>Tithi</note>, and the day of the week, various results arise through the combinations of these factors. Following this, one should consider the transition of the Sun <term>Sankranti: the movement of the Sun from one zodiac sign to another</term>, which also yields specific fruits. **9**

The following topics are also essential: the transits of planets <term>Gocara: the current positions of planets relative to one's birth chart</term>, the strength of the Moon <term>Shashi-bala</term>, the various rites of passage <term>Samskara: life-cycle rituals such as initiation or marriage</term>, the coronation of kings, the rules for departing on journeys, the construction of a residence, the ceremony for entering a new home, the wearing of new clothes, and the consecration of the gods. **10**

Thus ends the beautiful and pure *Jewel Garland of Auspicious Moments* <note>original: "Lalita Muhurta Nirmala Ratnamala"</note>, a complete treasury of jewels emerging from the ocean of knowledge. **Sripati**, who bows at the feet of his own venerable teacher, has composed this.

**Extent** 66 <note>This indicates the manuscript contains 66 folios (handwritten pages).</note> **Age** 1780 <note>This date is recorded in the Vikrama Samvat calendar, which corresponds to approximately 1723–1724 CE.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: "Jyotiṣam"; the traditional branch of Vedic science that encompasses both astronomical observation and the calculation of auspicious timing.</note>

# Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,
## Jammu

**No.** <term>856 — Gha</term> <note>The handwritten characters correspond to the manuscript’s unique classification and accession number within the archive's specific shelf or category "Gha" (घ).</note>

**Title** <term>The Jewel-Garland of Astrology</term> <note>original: "Jyotiṣaratnamālā"; a highly influential 11th-century Sanskrit manual on electional astrology (Muhurta), used for determining auspicious times for religious and social ceremonies.</note>

**Author** <term>Shripati Bhatta</term> <note>original: "Śrīpatibhaṭṭaḥ"; a celebrated Indian astronomer and mathematician whose works were standard textbooks in medieval India.</note>

**Extent** <term>66</term> <note>Refers to the total number of leaves (folios) in the manuscript.</note> **Age** <term>1780</term> <note>Dated in the Vikrama Samvat calendar system, which translates to approximately 1723–1724 CE.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: "Jyotiṣam"; the ancient Indian science that treats both astronomy and astrology as a single unified field.</note>

### Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library, Jammu

**No.** <term>858 — Gha</term> <note>The number 858 is the unique identifier for this manuscript, and "Gha" refers to the specific alphabetical shelf or section in the library's classification system.</note>

**Title** <term>The Jewel-Garland of Astrology</term> <note>original: "Jyotiṣaratnamālā"; a classic 11th-century manual on electional astrology (Muhurta), used to determine the most favorable times for various life events and rituals.</note>

**Author** <term>Shripati Bhatta</term> <note>original: "Śrīpatibhaṭṭaḥ"; a prominent Indian astronomer and mathematician whose works remained influential for centuries across the Indian subcontinent.</note>

**Extent** <term>66</term> <note>This indicates the manuscript contains 66 folios or leaves.</note> **Age** <term>1780</term> <note>Dated in the Vikrama Samvat calendar system, which is roughly 1723–1724 CE.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: "Jyotiṣam"; the traditional Indian science that encompasses both mathematical astronomy and predictive astrology.</note>
